_source line 909 · 2026-05-20T23:48:29.746Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QyfMbz9BcvHHS2NMoHqSVP`</summary>

````text
1	# Experiment Summary: IRT-Equated Benchmark Regeneration
2	
3	## Overview
4	
5	This project investigated whether Differential Item Functioning (DIF) analysis — a psychometric technique from educational testing — can detect benchmark contamination in LLMs. Starting from the hypothesis that MH-DIF could identify differentially contaminated items between temporal cohorts (2023 vs 2024 models), we ran a systematic chain of 11 experiments on the METABENCH dataset (5227 models × 12508 MMLU items).
6	
7	The core finding is a pivot: temporal DIF does not primarily detect contamination but reflects **genuine capability improvement** across model generations. Three independent approaches to reduce the 31% baseline DIF rate all failed (matched-ability, θ-conditioning, within-subject isolation), while semi-synthetic injection validated that the DIF methodology itself works correctly (ΔTPR=+0.674). The project pivoted from "DIF as contamination detector" to "psychometric characterization of LLM benchmark item instability" — the first large-scale quantification of how benchmark items behave differentially across model generations.
8	
9	## Experiment Chain
10	
11	### MVP Phase 1: DIF Power Simulation (Synthetic)
12	- **Exp ID**: mvp_dif_power_sim
13	- **Hypothesis**: MH + Logistic Regression DIF achieves TPR≥0.70, FPR≤0.10 at N≥50-80/group, δ≥1.0 logits on synthetic 2PL IRT data
14	- **Result**: BH-corrected p-value detection FAIL (TPR 0.04-0.07). ETS C classification conditional PASS: TPR=0.72-0.85 at N≥80, δ≥1.4; FPR<0.05; AUC=0.91-0.96
15	- **Conclusion**: Conditional success — ETS C replaces p-value as primary detector (D001)
16	- **Story contribution**: Established operational parameters (N≥80, δ≥1.4) and the ETS C classification as the primary DIF detection criterion
17	
18	### MVP Phase 2: DIF Power Simulation (Real IRT Parameters)
19	- **Exp ID**: mvp_dif_power_sim_phase2
20	- **Hypothesis**: ETS C maintains TPR≥0.65, FPR≤0.10 at N≥80, δ≥1.4 with real MMLU 2PL parameters from PSN-IRT
21	- **Result**: TPR=0.68 (N=80,δ=1.4) to 0.80 (N=100,δ=1.6); FPR=0.036-0.042; AUC=0.93
22	- **Conclusion**: Success — real parameter distributions degrade TPR by 3-6pp, within tolerance (D002)
23	- **Story contribution**: Validates DIF power under realistic item parameter distributions; recommends N≥100 as primary operating point
24	
25	### Plan 001: Real-Data MH-DIF on METABENCH MMLU
26	- **Exp ID**: plan_001_real_data_dif_mmlu
27	- **Hypothesis**: Temporal cohort MH-DIF flags items enriched in Li et al. contaminated set (enrichment >1.0, FPR≤0.05)
28	- **Result**: Temporal C%=31.3%, enrichment=0.96 (no enrichment); ability C%=2.9%, enrichment=1.19; within-family Llama-2 vs 3 C%=61.3%; domain ρ=0.307 (p=0.02); sanity random-split C%=0.18%
29	- **Conclusion**: Negative — temporal DIF detects systematic capability improvement, not contamination (D004)
30	- **Story contribution**: Key negative finding that motivated the entire pivot. The 31.3% temporal C% becomes the headline number for "item instability." Domain ρ=0.307 shows DIF is capability-driven. Sanity check (0.18%) proves the method isn't broken.
31	
32	### Plan 001b: Matched-Ability Temporal DIF
33	- **Exp ID**: plan_001b_matched_ability_dif
34	- **Hypothesis**: Within each accuracy quintile, temporal DIF enrichment rises to >1.5 after controlling for ability
35	- **Result**: All quintile enrichments 0.94-1.04
36	- **Conclusion**: Negative — ability matching cannot isolate contamination signal (D005)
37	- **Story contribution**: Eliminates "ability confound" as the sole explanation. Reviewer insight: Li et al. web-overlap is global contamination (available to all models), fundamentally incompatible with DIF which measures differential effects.
38	
39	### Plan 011: Semi-Synthetic DIF Validation
40	- **Exp ID**: plan_011_semi_synthetic_validation
41	- **Hypothesis**: MH-DIF detects injected differential contamination (focal-only incorrect→correct flips) with TPR>0.5, FPR<0.05 on ability cohort
42	- **Result**: Temporal+external: ΔTPR=+0.674 (0.326→1.000), FPR constant at 0.314; clear dose-response across p={0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0}; matching variable contamination discovered (standard MH FPR 0.298→0.616 at 20% injection, anchor/external immune)
43	- **Conclusion**: Success — DIF methodology validated; plan_001 failure attributed to ground truth mismatch (D006)
44	- **Story contribution**: The decisive validation experiment. Proves DIF works for differential contamination. Reinterprets Li et al. enrichment≈1.0 as "global vs differential contamination" insight (conceptual contribution). Matching variable contamination finding motivates external/anchor matching.
45	
46	### Plan 012: IRT θ-Conditioning
47	- **Exp ID**: plan_012_irt_theta_conditioning
48	- **Hypothesis**: Logit θ stratification (10-20 bins) reduces temporal baseline FPR from 31% to <10% while maintaining TPR>0.70
49	- **Result**: θ corr with total score = 0.9986; temporal FPR = 31-35% across all variants (no improvement); ability cohort θ-bins reduce FPR 49%→8% but TPR collapses 76%→21% (proving plan_011 ability TPR was mostly ability confounding)
50	- **Conclusion**: Negative — 1D θ ≡ total score, cannot reduce temporal FPR (D008)
51	- **Story contribution**: Proves temporal DIF is genuinely multidimensional — 1D ability cannot explain it. Corrects plan_011 ability cohort interpretation: 73% of apparent TPR was ability confounding.
52	
53	### Plan 013: Yen's Q3 Local Independence Diagnostic
54	- **Exp ID**: plan_013_q3_local_independence
55	- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject Q3 mean > 0.20, significantly higher than cross-subject, explaining part of 31% FPR
56	- **Result**: Within-subject mean Q3=0.0086 (below threshold), but 11.3% of pairs |Q3|>0.20 (vs 2.2% cross-subject, 5x ratio, Mann-Whitney p=2.3e-9); worst subjects: moral_scenarios 34.2%, world_religions 18%, marketing 17.5%
57	- **Conclusion**: Partial — local dependence exists but is moderate; not the primary driver of 31% FPR
58	- **Story contribution**: Addresses reviewer concern about IRT assumptions. Local dependence is a contributing factor but insufficient to explain 31% FPR, strengthening the "genuine temporal DIF" interpretation. Identifies specific problematic subjects for Discussion section.
59	
60	### Plan 014: Within-Subject Temporal DIF (Bifactor Pre-Check)
61	- **Exp ID**: plan_014_within_subject_dif
62	- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject C% < 15% in high-C% subjects, indicating between-subject ability differences drive overall C%
63	- **Result**: Average within-subject C%=38.1% (range 31.1-45.2%); cross-subject C%=40.5% (Δ=-2.4pp); all 5 subjects exceed 25% threshold
64	- **Conclusion**: Negative — temporal DIF penetrates into every subject; bifactor model cannot solve FPR (D010)
65	- **Story contribution**: Proves temporal DIF is pervasive and item-specific, not an artifact of between-subject ability differences. The 38.1% within-subject figure is a robustness check for the 31.3% global finding. Kills bifactor direction before wasting compute.
66	
67	### Plan 015: Bifactor IRT (Killed)
68	- **Exp ID**: plan_015_bifactor_irt
69	- **Hypothesis**: Bifactor (1 general + 57 subject factors) reduces temporal FPR to <15%
70	- **Conclusion**: Killed based on plan_014 pre-check (D010) — within-subject C%=38.1% proves bifactor cannot solve the problem
71	- **Story contribution**: Demonstrates methodological rigor — pre-mortem analysis saved compute and confirmed the exhaustive nature of the investigation
72	
73	### Plan 016: Within-Subject Semi-Synthetic Injection
74	- **Exp ID**: plan_016_within_subject_semi_synthetic
75	- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject DIF still detects injected contamination (ΔTPR>0.3 at p=0.5), validating plan_014's 38% as genuine
76	- **Result**: Cross-subject matching 4/5 subjects ΔTPR>0.3 at p=0.5; ΔFPR=0.000 across all 5 subjects (completely stable); within-subject matching 3/5 pass but FPR inflates +0.067 to +0.281
77	- **Conclusion**: Success — DIF works at subject level; cross-subject matching eliminates FPR inflation (D011)
78	- **Story contribution**: Final validation. Cross-subject ΔFPR=0.000 is a clean methodological contribution (solves matching variable contamination at subject level). Confirms plan_014's 38% is real temporal DIF, not method failure.
79	
80	### Plan 017: Downstream Impact (Preparing)
81	- **Exp ID**: plan_017_downstream_impact
82	- **Hypothesis**: Removing DIF-flagged items (~31% of MMLU) improves cross-generation model ranking stability (Spearman ρ), significantly better than random removal baseline
83	- **Status**: PREPARING
84	- **Story contribution**: Answers the "so what?" question — if DIF-flagged items cause ranking instability, removing them improves benchmark quality, providing downstream evidence for DIF as a benchmark quality tool
85	
86	## Decision Chain (D001-D012)
87	
88	```
89	D001 (ETS C > p-value) → D002 (MVP pass) → D003 (Dream #1)
90	    → D004 (temporal DIF = capability, not contamination)
91	        → D005 (matched-ability fails)
92	            → D006 (semi-synthetic validates DIF method)
93	                → D007 (story pivot: global vs differential contamination)
94	                    → D008 (θ-conditioning fails, 1D exhausted)
95	                        → D009 (Dream #2: bifactor is last hope)
96	                            → D010 (within-subject C%=38%, bifactor killed)
97	                                → D011 (pivot confirmed, cross-matching ΔFPR=0.000)
98	                                    → D012 (story finalized: benchmark quality tool)
99	```
100	
101	Three failed remediation directions:
102	1. **D005**: Matched-ability DIF → enrichment still ~1.0
103	2. **D008**: IRT θ-conditioning → θ ≡ total score (r=0.9986), FPR unchanged
104	3. **D010**: Bifactor pre-check → within-subject C%=38.1% > overall 31.3%
105	
106	## Key Numbers for Paper
107	
108	| Metric | Value | Source | Paper use |
109	|--------|-------|--------|-----------|
110	| METABENCH dataset size | 5227 models × 12508 items | data | Section 3 (Data) |
111	| Temporal C% (ETS C flagged) | 31.3% | plan_001 | Key finding: item instability rate |
112	| Ability C% | 2.9% | plan_001 | Contrast: ability-based DIF well-controlled |
113	| Li et al. enrichment (temporal) | 0.96 | plan_001 | Global vs differential contamination |
114	| Li et al. enrichment (ability) | 1.19 | plan_001 | Weak positive in ability cohort |
115	| Within-family C% (Llama-2 vs 3) | 61.3% | plan_001 | Impact-vs-DIF confound demonstration |
116	| Domain specificity ρ(C%, ΔAcc) | 0.307 (p=0.02) | plan_001 | Capability-driven DIF evidence |
117	| Sanity check C% (random split) | 0.18% | plan_001 | Method validity |
118	| Semi-synthetic ΔTPR (temporal, p=1.0) | +0.674 | plan_011 | Core validation: DIF detects differential contamination |
119	| Semi-synthetic FPR stability | 0.314 → 0.314 | plan_011 | FPR constant under injection |
120	| Semi-synthetic dose-response p=0.5 | TPR=0.834 | plan_011 | Detection at moderate effect |
121	| Matching variable contamination ΔFPR | 0.298 → 0.616 | plan_011 | Motivates external matching |
122	| θ–total score correlation | 0.9986 | plan_012 | 1D θ ≡ total score |
123	| Ability cohort corrected TPR (θ-bins) | 21% (was 76%) | plan_012 | 73% of ability TPR was confounding |
124	| Q3 within-subject |Q3|>0.20 | 11.3% (vs 2.2% cross) | plan_013 | Local dependence: moderate, not primary |
125	| Worst Q3 subject (moral_scenarios) | 34.2% pairs |Q3|>0.20 | plan_013 | Discussion: problematic subjects |
126	| Within-subject temporal C% (average) | 38.1% (range 31-45%) | plan_014 | Robustness: DIF pervasive within domains |
127	| Within vs cross-subject Δ | -2.4pp | plan_014 | Signal robust to matching choice |
128	| Cross-matching ΔFPR (all subjects) | 0.000 | plan_016 | Method: FPR completely stable |
129	| Cross-matching subjects with ΔTPR>0.3 | 4/5 at p=0.5 | plan_016 | Subject-level detection works |
130	| Within-matching FPR inflation at p=1.0 | +0.067 to +0.281 | plan_016 | Motivates cross-matching |
131	| MVP ETS C TPR at N=100, δ=1.6 | 0.80 | mvp_phase2 | Power analysis: method is powered |
132	| MVP ETS C FPR range | 0.036-0.042 | mvp_phase2 | Controlled false positive rate |
133	
134	## Evidence Chain Summary
135	
136	**Conclusion: Temporal DIF = genuine capability improvement, not contamination artifact.**
137	
138	The evidence chain:
139	
140	1. **DIF works** (plan_011): Semi-synthetic injection with known differential contamination yields ΔTPR=+0.674, perfect dose-response, and constant FPR — the methodology is sound.
141	
142	2. **But temporal DIF doesn't align with contamination labels** (plan_001): Enrichment=0.96 against Li et al. web-overlap labels. This is because web-overlap is *global* contamination (available to all models via Common Crawl), which by definition cannot produce group-specific DIF.
143	
144	3. **31% is not an ability-matching artifact**:
145	   - Matched-ability DIF still shows enrichment≈1.0 (plan_001b)
146	   - IRT θ is indistinguishable from total score (r=0.9986), finer binning doesn't help (plan_012)
147	   - Within-subject C%=38.1% — higher than global 31.3%, ruling out between-subject confounds (plan_014)
148	   - Local dependence (Q3) is moderate (11.3% >0.20) but insufficient to explain 31% (plan_013)
149	
150	4. **The method is validated at subject level** (plan_016): Cross-subject matching achieves ΔFPR=0.000 across all 5 tested subjects, with 4/5 showing ΔTPR>0.3 at p=0.5 injection level.
151	
152	5. **Therefore**: 31-38% of MMLU items exhibit genuine differential functioning between 2023 and 2024 model generations. This reflects real, item-specific capability changes — not contamination, not statistical artifacts, not matching failures. DIF is a valid tool for characterizing benchmark item instability, complementing but distinct from contamination detection.

      "content": "# Experiment Summary: IRT-Equated Benchmark Regeneration\n\n## Overview\n\nThis project investigated whether Differential Item Functioning (DIF) analysis — a psychometric technique from educational testing — can detect benchmark contamination in LLMs. Starting from the hypothesis that MH-DIF could identify differentially contaminated items between temporal cohorts (2023 vs 2024 models), we ran a systematic chain of 11 experiments on the METABENCH dataset (5227 models × 12508 MMLU items).\n\nThe core finding is a pivot: temporal DIF does not primarily detect contamination but reflects **genuine capability improvement** across model generations. Three independent approaches to reduce the 31% baseline DIF rate all failed (matched-ability, θ-conditioning, within-subject isolation), while semi-synthetic injection validated that the DIF methodology itself works correctly (ΔTPR=+0.674). The project pivoted from \"DIF as contamination detector\" to \"psychometric characterization of LLM benchmark item instability\" — the first large-scale quantification of how benchmark items behave differentially across model generations.\n\n## Experiment Chain\n\n### MVP Phase 1: DIF Power Simulation (Synthetic)\n- **Exp ID**: mvp_dif_power_sim\n- **Hypothesis**: MH + Logistic Regression DIF achieves TPR≥0.70, FPR≤0.10 at N≥50-80/group, δ≥1.0 logits on synthetic 2PL IRT data\n- **Result**: BH-corrected p-value detection FAIL (TPR 0.04-0.07). ETS C classification conditional PASS: TPR=0.72-0.85 at N≥80, δ≥1.4; FPR<0.05; AUC=0.91-0.96\n- **Conclusion**: Conditional success — ETS C replaces p-value as primary detector (D001)\n- **Story contribution**: Established operational parameters (N≥80, δ≥1.4) and the ETS C classification as the primary DIF detection criterion\n\n### MVP Phase 2: DIF Power Simulation (Real IRT Parameters)\n- **Exp ID**: mvp_dif_power_sim_phase2\n- **Hypothesis**: ETS C maintains TPR≥0.65, FPR≤0.10 at N≥80, δ≥1.4 with real MMLU 2PL parameters from PSN-IRT\n- **Result**: TPR=0.68 (N=80,δ=1.4) to 0.80 (N=100,δ=1.6); FPR=0.036-0.042; AUC=0.93\n- **Conclusion**: Success — real parameter distributions degrade TPR by 3-6pp, within tolerance (D002)\n- **Story contribution**: Validates DIF power under realistic item parameter distributions; recommends N≥100 as primary operating point\n\n### Plan 001: Real-Data MH-DIF on METABENCH MMLU\n- **Exp ID**: plan_001_real_data_dif_mmlu\n- **Hypothesis**: Temporal cohort MH-DIF flags items enriched in Li et al. contaminated set (enrichment >1.0, FPR≤0.05)\n- **Result**: Temporal C%=31.3%, enrichment=0.96 (no enrichment); ability C%=2.9%, enrichment=1.19; within-family Llama-2 vs 3 C%=61.3%; domain ρ=0.307 (p=0.02); sanity random-split C%=0.18%\n- **Conclusion**: Negative — temporal DIF detects systematic capability improvement, not contamination (D004)\n- **Story contribution**: Key negative finding that motivated the entire pivot. The 31.3% temporal C% becomes the headline number for \"item instability.\" Domain ρ=0.307 shows DIF is capability-driven. Sanity check (0.18%) proves the method isn't broken.\n\n### Plan 001b: Matched-Ability Temporal DIF\n- **Exp ID**: plan_001b_matched_ability_dif\n- **Hypothesis**: Within each accuracy quintile, temporal DIF enrichment rises to >1.5 after controlling for ability\n- **Result**: All quintile enrichments 0.94-1.04\n- **Conclusion**: Negative — ability matching cannot isolate contamination signal (D005)\n- **Story contribution**: Eliminates \"ability confound\" as the sole explanation. Reviewer insight: Li et al. web-overlap is global contamination (available to all models), fundamentally incompatible with DIF which measures differential effects.\n\n### Plan 011: Semi-Synthetic DIF Validation\n- **Exp ID**: plan_011_semi_synthetic_validation\n- **Hypothesis**: MH-DIF detects injected differential contamination (focal-only incorrect→correct flips) with TPR>0.5, FPR<0.05 on ability cohort\n- **Result**: Temporal+external: ΔTPR=+0.674 (0.326→1.000), FPR constant at 0.314; clear dose-response across p={0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0}; matching variable contamination discovered (standard MH FPR 0.298→0.616 at 20% injection, anchor/external immune)\n- **Conclusion**: Success — DIF methodology validated; plan_001 failure attributed to ground truth mismatch (D006)\n- **Story contribution**: The decisive validation experiment. Proves DIF works for differential contamination. Reinterprets Li et al. enrichment≈1.0 as \"global vs differential contamination\" insight (conceptual contribution). Matching variable contamination finding motivates external/anchor matching.\n\n### Plan 012: IRT θ-Conditioning\n- **Exp ID**: plan_012_irt_theta_conditioning\n- **Hypothesis**: Logit θ stratification (10-20 bins) reduces temporal baseline FPR from 31% to <10% while maintaining TPR>0.70\n- **Result**: θ corr with total score = 0.9986; temporal FPR = 31-35% across all variants (no improvement); ability cohort θ-bins reduce FPR 49%→8% but TPR collapses 76%→21% (proving plan_011 ability TPR was mostly ability confounding)\n- **Conclusion**: Negative — 1D θ ≡ total score, cannot reduce temporal FPR (D008)\n- **Story contribution**: Proves temporal DIF is genuinely multidimensional — 1D ability cannot explain it. Corrects plan_011 ability cohort interpretation: 73% of apparent TPR was ability confounding.\n\n### Plan 013: Yen's Q3 Local Independence Diagnostic\n- **Exp ID**: plan_013_q3_local_independence\n- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject Q3 mean > 0.20, significantly higher than cross-subject, explaining part of 31% FPR\n- **Result**: Within-subject mean Q3=0.0086 (below threshold), but 11.3% of pairs |Q3|>0.20 (vs 2.2% cross-subject, 5x ratio, Mann-Whitney p=2.3e-9); worst subjects: moral_scenarios 34.2%, world_religions 18%, marketing 17.5%\n- **Conclusion**: Partial — local dependence exists but is moderate; not the primary driver of 31% FPR\n- **Story contribution**: Addresses reviewer concern about IRT assumptions. Local dependence is a contributing factor but insufficient to explain 31% FPR, strengthening the \"genuine temporal DIF\" interpretation. Identifies specific problematic subjects for Discussion section.\n\n### Plan 014: Within-Subject Temporal DIF (Bifactor Pre-Check)\n- **Exp ID**: plan_014_within_subject_dif\n- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject C% < 15% in high-C% subjects, indicating between-subject ability differences drive overall C%\n- **Result**: Average within-subject C%=38.1% (range 31.1-45.2%); cross-subject C%=40.5% (Δ=-2.4pp); all 5 subjects exceed 25% threshold\n- **Conclusion**: Negative — temporal DIF penetrates into every subject; bifactor model cannot solve FPR (D010)\n- **Story contribution**: Proves temporal DIF is pervasive and item-specific, not an artifact of between-subject ability differences. The 38.1% within-subject figure is a robustness check for the 31.3% global finding. Kills bifactor direction before wasting compute.\n\n### Plan 015: Bifactor IRT (Killed)\n- **Exp ID**: plan_015_bifactor_irt\n- **Hypothesis**: Bifactor (1 general + 57 subject factors) reduces temporal FPR to <15%\n- **Conclusion**: Killed based on plan_014 pre-check (D010) — within-subject C%=38.1% proves bifactor cannot solve the problem\n- **Story contribution**: Demonstrates methodological rigor — pre-mortem analysis saved compute and confirmed the exhaustive nature of the investigation\n\n### Plan 016: Within-Subject Semi-Synthetic Injection\n- **Exp ID**: plan_016_within_subject_semi_synthetic\n- **Hypothesis**: Within-subject DIF still detects injected contamination (ΔTPR>0.3 at p=0.5), validating plan_014's 38% as genuine\n- **Result**: Cross-subject matching 4/5 subjects ΔTPR>0.3 at p=0.5; ΔFPR=0.000 across all 5 subjects (completely stable); within-subject matching 3/5 pass but FPR inflates +0.067 to +0.281\n- **Conclusion**: Success — DIF works at subject level; cross-subject matching eliminates FPR inflation (D011)\n- **Story contribution**: Final validation. Cross-subject ΔFPR=0.000 is a clean methodological contribution (solves matching variable contamination at subject level). Confirms plan_014's 38% is real temporal DIF, not method failure.\n\n### Plan 017: Downstream Impact (Preparing)\n- **Exp ID**: plan_017_downstream_impact\n- **Hypothesis**: Removing DIF-flagged items (~31% of MMLU) improves cross-generation model ranking stability (Spearman ρ), significantly better than random removal baseline\n- **Status**: PREPARING\n- **Story contribution**: Answers the \"so what?\" question — if DIF-flagged items cause ranking instability, removing them improves benchmark quality, providing downstream evidence for DIF as a benchmark quality tool\n\n## Decision Chain One thing I'm realizing though: the summary frames this as purely "genuine capability improvement," but we can't actually rule out that some portion of that 31-38% could be differential contamination. We know DIF doesn't correlate with Li et al.'s global contamination labels, but those measure something different than differential contamination, and we don't have ground truth for that. So the more precise statement would be that 31-38% reflects capability changes and/or differential contamination—we just can't separate them without better data. It's a minor overstatement rather than a fundamental error.

I should verify whether plan_017 exists in the registry and double-check the D012 reference.

1	{
2	  "raw": "plan_017 完成。6 个 artifact 已注册。\n\n## 核心发现\n\n**1. 排名高度鲁棒**：移除 31.3% DIF items 后，模型排名与 full benchmark 的 Spearman ρ ≈ 0.996-0.999。\n\n**2. DIF items 不是噪声——它们携带浓缩的差异化信息**：\n- DIF-based removal 在所有 21/21 条件下造成的排名扰动都**显著大于**随机移除（z = -8 到 -49σ）\n- 这意味着 DIF items 系统性地区分了模型，不是随机噪声\n\n**3. DIF items 捕捉了时间不稳定性**：\n- 跨代 super-family 比较（8 个 family：llama, qwen, mistral 等）中，DIF items 的准确率变化幅度是 full benchmark 的 **1.09×**，stable items 仅 **0.96×**\n\n**4. 排名变动最大的模型**：\n- 从 DIF items 获益最多（移除后排名下降最大）：phi-3、llama-3 衍生模型、一些 14B+ 模型（Δrank +700-850）\n- 被 DIF items 压制（移除后排名上升最大）：mistral 系 merge 模型（Δrank -400 to -587）\n\n**论文叙事角度**：DIF items 不是可以简单丢弃的\"坏题\"——它们是对不同时代模型有差异化效应的题目。移除它们虽然在跨代准确率比较上产生小幅稳定效果（~4%），但会显著改变模型排名，表明这些 items 承载了关于模型能力差异的结构化信息。"
3	}
```

_source line 958 · 2026-05-21T00:04:34.299Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very interesting result. Plan_017 is the downstream impact experiment I recommended in my Framing review. Let me read the full report.

Key findings:
1. Removing DIF items barely changes rankings (ρ ≈ 0.996-0.999) — very high stability
2. But DIF items are NOT noise — removal causes significantly MORE ranking disruption than random removal (z=-8 to -49σ)
3. DIF items capture temporal instability (1.09× accuracy change vs 0.96× for stable items)
4. Specific models affected: phi-3, llama-3 derivatives go down; mistral merges go up


5. The high correlation despite removing a third of items suggests MMLU has enough redundancy that losing that portion doesn't substantially alter the overall rankings, yet the DIF items themselves encode systematic differences between models that random items wouldn't capture — they're concentrating where temporal variation actually occurs.

This creates an interesting tension for the benchmark quality framing: if DIF items barely shift rankings but carry structured information about model differences, it suggests they're simultaneously redundant for ranking purposes and essential for understanding what distinguishes models across time.

Let me check the full report to verify these numbers and understand the methodology better.
```

_source line 966 · 2026-05-21T00:05:12.242Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very well-designed experiment. Let me analyze the key findings:

**Part 1: Full vs Stable-only Ranking**
- ρ(full, stable) = 0.996-0.999 → removing 31% DIF items barely changes rankings
- Split-half reliability barely changes: full 0.9934 vs stable 0.9919

**Part 2: Progressive Removal**
- Smooth degradation as more items removed
- Even at 38% removal, ρ > 0.995 for all cohorts
- This confirms MMLU has high redundancy
  
**Part 3: DIF vs Random Removal**
- DIF items consistently disrupt rankings more than random removal across all conditions, which suggests they're encoding systematic information rather than noise
- This complicates the narrative around simply removing DIF items to improve the benchmark

**Part 4: Top Rank Movers**
- Newer, more capable models like phi-3 and llama-3 derivatives lose ground when DIF items are removed, while older merge-based models actually improve
- The pattern indicates that DIF items systematically advantage the latest generation of models

**Part 5: Cross-Cohort Accuracy Shifts**
- DIF items show a 1.09× amplification in accuracy shifts compared to stable items, confirming they're where temporal differences concentrate, though the effect is modest
- The key tension is that while DIF items clearly capture genuine instability, removing them doesn't meaningfully reorder the rankings and actually causes more disruption than random removal, suggesting they're informative rather than problematic for the benchmark itself

Now I'm checking the methodological details—how stable items were defined using the temporal cohort classification, whether the random baseline properly matched removal sizes with multiple seeds, and noting that some model families have very imbalanced representation across years which could introduce noise into the cross-cohort estimates. I should also verify how the reliability metric was calculated and flag potential issues with family classification quality given the uneven sample sizes.

The rank movers table shows most top-50 models classified as "unknown" family, which undermines the family-level interpretability. Falcon and hermes have dangerously small sample sizes in 2024—just one and four models respectively—making their accuracy estimates unreliable. The near-perfect correlation of 0.997 actually suggests MMLU has built-in redundancy that makes it robust regardless of which items are removed, which somewhat contradicts the framing that removing DIF items meaningfully improves the benchmark.

The real insight here is that DIF items aren't flawed items worth removing—they're diagnostic markers showing where model capabilities shifted most over time. The 1.09× amplification in temporal variation is modest, only 9% above average, so while statistically significant, it's not dramatically different. This reframes the contribution from a "quality tool" angle toward a "characterization tool" that reveals genuine model evolution.

Let me check the registry status to make sure everything's consistent.
```

```text
plan_017 is RUNNING with conclusion "negative". The worker has completed but the Agent hasn't updated the status to DONE yet. The conclusion is "negative" — let me look at this more carefully.

The conclusion being "negative" makes sense from the original hypothesis perspective: "removing DIF-flagged items (~31% of MMLU) improves cross-generation model ranking stability (Spearman ρ), significantly better than random removal baseline." The result showed ρ≈0.997 (barely changes) AND DIF removal causes MORE disruption than random (z=-8 to -49). So the hypothesis that removal "improves" ranking stability is technically rejected — removing DIF items actually makes rankings LESS stable compared to random removal.
 
Even though the hypothesis didn't pan out, these findings are actually quite valuable for the paper's narrative. The key insight is that DIF items contain structured information that matters for model ranking stability, which shifts the framing from a "benchmark quality tool" toward a "characterization" angle. I'm noticing some methodological constraints though — the super-family sample sizes are tiny (falcon N=1), and 49 out of 50 top rank movers fall into the "unknown" family category, which suggests I need a more nuanced interpretation of what's really happening here.
```

{
  "text": "## 审查报告 — plan_017 Downstream Impact Analysis\n\n**结论**: [severity: warning] 实验执行正确，数据可靠，但结果对 story 有重大影响——**削弱 \"benchmark quality tool\" framing，加强 \"characterization\" framing**。需要调整叙事。\n\n### 核心发现审查\n\n**数据验证**: ranking_comparison.csv / random_baseline.csv 数据一致，ρ 值、CI、z-score 格式和量级合理。✓\n\n**关键结果**:\n1. ρ(full, stable) = 0.996-0.999 → 移除 31% DIF items 对排名的影响微乎其微\n2. DIF removal 在 21/21 条件下比 random removal 造成**更大**排名扰动 (z = -8 到 -49σ)\n3. DIF items 的跨代准确率变化幅度是 full 的 1.09×（modest amplification）\n\n### 对 story 的关键影响\n\n**plan_017 的结果本质上否定了 Candidate 1（\"benchmark quality tool\"）的核心叙事**。\n\n我在 Framing 审查中问的 \"so what?\" 问题现在有了答案，但答案不利于 \"quality tool\" framing：\n\n- ❌ \"移除 DIF items 改善 benchmark 质量\" → 排名几乎不变 (ρ≈0.997)，无可测量的\"改善\"\n- ❌ \"DIF items 是低质量的噪声 items\" → DIF removal > random removal (z=-14.91 avg)，说明 DIF items 携带**更多**结构化信息，不是噪声\n- ✅ \"DIF items 是模型代际差异集中的 items\" → 1.09× amplification + phi-3/llama-3 受益最大\n\n**正确叙事**: DIF items 不是应该被移除的\"坏题\"——它们是模型能力差异的**放大镜**。Temporal DIF 准确地识别了那些在模型代际间功能变化最大的 items。这是 DIF 作为 **diagnostic tool**（诊断哪些 items 在变）而非 **quality control tool**（找出应移除的 items）的证据。\n\n### 方法论问题\n\n1. **Super-family 样本量不足**：falcon N24=1, hermes N24=4, wizardlm N24=4。单个模型的 Δacc 不可靠。报告未注明此 caveat。论文如果引用 super-family 结果，需限制在 N≥20 的 families（llama, mistral, qwen, phi）。\n\n2. **Family 分类覆盖低**：Top-50 rank movers 中 49 个标记为 \"unknown\" family。虽然 phi-3 和 llama-3 的模式清晰，但 \"unknown\" 占绝大多数削弱了 family-level 分析的可信度。\n\n3. **Registry 状态**：plan_017 仍为 RUNNING，应更新为 DONE。\n\n### 建议\n\n**Story 方向调整**：放弃 \"remove DIF items to improve quality\"，改为：\n- DIF 作为 **benchmark diagnostic** — 识别哪些 items 在模型代际间功能最不稳定\n- ρ≈0.997 本身是发现：**MMLU 对 item removal 高度鲁棒**（31% 的 items 可移除且排名几乎不变）→ benchmark redundancy characterization\n- DIF items 携带关于模型差异的浓缩信息 → 可用于理解模型能力变化的**哪些维度**在驱动排名\n- 与 Growing Pains 的互补叙事强化：\"他们用 anchor items 做高效评估，我们的 DIF 诊断告诉他们哪些 anchor items 正在失效\"",

_source line 1010 · 2026-05-21T00:07:59.905Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The Director is asking me to evaluate how plan_017's "inverted" result affects the ICLR viability assessment. Let me think through this carefully.

My previous assessment said ICLR 2027 needs at least one of:
1. Cross-benchmark validation (GSM8K/GSM1K)
2. Downstream impact (remove DIF items → ranking stability improves)
3. Detection trilemma formal result

Now plan_017 shows:
- Removing DIF items does NOT improve rankings (ρ≈0.997, barely changes)
- DIF removal causes MORE disruption than random (z=-8 to -49σ)


- DIF items carry concentrated information about model differences

So the inverted finding actually does answer the "so what?" question that downstream impact was supposed to address — it just flips the narrative. Rather than showing that removing DIF items helps practitioners, it reveals that DIF items are where the real temporal variation lives, and MMLU itself is largely redundant noise. This reframes the contribution from "here's what to filter out" to "here's where the meaningful signal concentrates."

The tension is that this is genuinely insightful — it tells practitioners that MMLU rankings are stable regardless of composition, and that DIF items are the diagnostic lens for understanding how models actually diverge across generations. But reviewers might push back that insight alone isn't enough without a concrete tool or actionable recommendation attached to it.

For ICLR 2027, the paper has solid methodological foundations and a novel application, but the downstream contribution is backwards — it's informative rather than prescriptive. The missing pieces are cross-benchmark validation, formal impossibility results, and something practitioners can actually *do* with the findings beyond "look at DIF items to understand model differences." The findings track rating of 6-7 seems right, possibly pushing toward 7 given how the inverted finding strengthens the narrative. For ICLR main, the real opportunity is validating this across GSM8K—if the DIF analysis shows contamination-enriched items or confirms the same pattern as MMLU, that would be the first real-world validation of DIF for contamination detection and fundamentally reshape the paper's contribution. Even if it just confirms the finding generalizes, that cross-benchmark validation is high-value and relatively cheap to run, making it the most important remaining experiment.

Stepping back on the ICLR assessment itself, I'm counting eight distinct contributions now: the large-scale psychometric analysis across 227 models and 12k items, the semi-synthetic validation framework, the global versus differential contamination distinction, the empirical detection trilemma, the cross-subject matching methodology with near-zero FPR difference, the temporal instability characterization, the inverted finding about DIF items as information carriers, and the MMLU redundancy result. That's substantial, though many are incremental. The question is whether they cohere into something compelling enough for ICLR, and I'm comparing against recent benchmark papers like Growing Pains with its practical evaluation tool and METABENCH's sparse approach.

The real tension is that our work is characterization rather than tooling, which seems to be where the field is moving. But there's a precedent for pure empirical findings that reshape thinking—like the emergent abilities paper—and our 31-38% temporal instability across MMLU could potentially do that. The problem is it's not quite surprising enough; most researchers already suspect benchmark comparability isn't perfect across generations. The inverted finding adds intrigue, but it doesn't shift the venue calculus. Cross-benchmark validation is where the real value lies.
```

{
  "text": "## Framing 补充评估 — plan_017 inverted result 对 ICLR 可行性的影响\n\n---\n\n### Q1: Inverted finding 是否算 \"downstream impact\" 的替代满足？\n\n**部分满足，但不完全。**\n\n原始需求是回答 \"so what?\"：如果 38% items temporally unstable，实践者该怎么做？\n\nplan_017 的回答是：\n- ✅ DIF items 不是噪声，而是差异化信号载体 → **理解模型差异时应关注 DIF items**\n- ✅ MMLU 对 item 移除高度鲁棒 (ρ≈0.997) → **benchmark redundancy characterization**\n- ✅ 特定 model families (phi-3, llama-3) 是 DIF items 的主要受益者 → **model-level insight**\n- ❌ 但没有 **actionable recommendation** — \"看 DIF items 来理解模型差异\" 不是工具，是建议\n\n对比：如果是 positive result（移除 DIF → 排名更稳定），实践者可以直接用。Inverted result 有科学价值（surprising finding > confirming expectation），但不产生可交付工具。\n\n**结论**：从科学角度，inverted finding 比 positive 更有信息量。但从 \"actionable output\" 角度，仍缺一步。它把 \"downstream impact\" 从 ❌ 提升到 ⚠️，不是 ✅。\n\n---\n\n### Q2: ICLR 2027 主会 vs findings track 判断是否改变？\n\n**不改变。**\n\n现有贡献清单（8 项）：\n1. 首次大规模 LLM benchmark DIF 分析\n2. Semi-synthetic validation framework\n3. Global vs differential contamination 区分\n4. Detection trilemma（empirical）\n5. Cross-subject matching (ΔFPR=0.000)\n6. 38% temporal item instability\n7. DIF items = structured info carriers（plan_017 inverted）\n8. MMLU redundancy (ρ≈0.997 after 31% removal)\n\n这些贡献数量充足，但每个单独偏 incremental。关键差距：**无 practical tool**。\n\n对标近年 ICLR/NeurIPS 评估类 accepted papers：\n- Growing Pains: FPC → 实践工具\n- METABENCH: sparse benchmark → 实践工具\n- tinyBenchmarks: IRT-based 高效评估 → 实践工具\n\n趋势是 **tool papers**，不是 characterization papers。我们的 paper 是 characterization。\n\n有一类例外：\"改变社区认知的 empirical findings paper\"（如 \"Are Emergent Abilities a Mirage?\"）。但那种 paper 需要 paradigm-shifting 发现。\"38% MMLU items 不稳定\" 是 informative but not surprising — 多数人已直觉性地知道跨代际 benchmark 分数不完全可比。\n\n**维持评估**：ICLR 主会 5.5-6 分（borderline），findings track 6.5-7 分（solid accept）。\n\n---\n\n### Q3: 是否仍需 cross-benchmark (plan_004 GSM8K)？\n\n**强烈建议做，这是 ROI 最高的剩余实验。**\n\n原因：\n\n1. **唯一可能找到 real-world contamination validation 的路径**：GSM1K 有独立的 contamination ground truth（Scale AI 构建的 GSM8K 平行版本，模型在 GSM8K 上的表现 vs GSM1K 的 accuracy gap 直接量化 contamination effect）。如果 DIF 在 GSM8K 上的 flags 与 GSM8K-GSM1K gap 相关，这将是**首次 DIF 真实世界污染检测验证** → 彻底改变论文性质，从 characterization 升级为 validated method。\n\n2. **Generalizability**：目前所有发现仅基于 MMLU/METABENCH。审稿人必问 \"does this generalize?\" 即使 GSM8K 结果与 MMLU 一致（temporal DIF = capability improvement），也回答了 generalization 问题。\n\n3. **成本极低**：CPU only，同一方法论（MH-DIF + semi-synthetic），主要工作是构建 GSM8K response matrix。如果 METABENCH 包含 GSM8K 数据（它确实包含），数据已在手。\n\n4. **两种结果都有价值**：\n   - DIF 与 GSM1K gap 相关 → **breakthrough**：DIF 确实检测到 contamination → ICLR 主会有戏\n   - DIF 与 GSM1K gap 不相关 → 加强 \"DIF = capability, not contamination\" narrative + generalization evidence\n\n**如果只能做一个额外实验，就做这个。**\n\n---\n\n### 总结\n\n| 问题 | 答案 |\n|------|------|\n| Inverted finding 替代 downstream impact？ | 部分（⚠️），有科学价值但缺 actionable output |\n| ICLR vs findings 判断改变？ | 不变：主会 borderline，findings solid |\n| 需要 cross-benchmark？ | **是，强烈建议**——唯一可能实现 breakthrough 的路径 |",

```text
1	# plan_004 Data Availability Report
2	
3	Date: 2026-05-21
4	
5	## METABENCH Coverage
6	
7	METABENCH (Kipnis et al., ICLR 2025) 包含 Open LLM Leaderboard 的全部 6 个 benchmarks，数据格式为 per-item binary responses (source, item, correct)。
8	
9	| Benchmark   | Items   | Models | Total Rows  |
10	|-------------|---------|--------|-------------|
11	| GSM8K       | 1,319   | 6,702  | 8,839,939   |
12	| ARC         | 1,172   | 5,227  | 6,126,045   |
13	| HellaSwag   | 10,042  | 5,227  | 52,489,535  |
14	| TruthfulQA  | 817     | 5,228  | 4,271,277   |
15	| Winogrande  | 1,267   | 6,558  | 8,308,987   |
16	| MMLU (57 subjects) | ~14,000+ | ~5,000+ | 74,012,560 |
17	
18	- **GSM8K present: YES**
19	- GSM8K items: 1,319 (full test set)
20	- GSM8K models: 6,702 (覆盖面极广，包含 Yi, Phi, Mistral, Llama, Qwen 等主流家族)
21	- 数据位置: `artifacts/metabench_data/benchmark-data/gsm8k.csv`
22	- Prompts: `artifacts/metabench_data/benchmark-data/gsm8k_prompts.csv`
23	
24	## GSM1K Availability
25	
26	| 数据类型 | 可用性 | 来源 |
27	|---------|--------|------|
28	| 题目文本 (1,205 problems) | ✅ 公开 | [HuggingFace ScaleAI/gsm1k](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ScaleAI/gsm1k) |
29	| Per-model accuracy (~30-50 models) | ✅ 公开 | [论文 arXiv:2405.00332](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00332) + [Scale Labs Leaderboard](https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/math) |
30	| Per-item binary responses (thousands of models) | ❌ 不公开 | Scale AI 内部评测，未公开 response matrix |
31	
32	关键限制：GSM1K 没有像 METABENCH 那样的大规模 per-item response matrix。论文只公布了约 30-50 个模型的聚合准确率。
33	
34	## Feasibility Assessment
35	
36	### Can proceed immediately: YES
37	
38	METABENCH 的 GSM8K 数据完全满足 IRT 分析需求：
39	- 1,319 items × 6,702 models 的完整 binary response matrix
40	- 同时拥有 5 个其他 benchmark 的同等数据
41	
42	### Cross-Benchmark Validation 方案
43	
44	**方案 A: 内部交叉验证（推荐，无需额外数据）**
45	- 用 METABENCH 内部的 6 个 benchmark 做交叉验证
46	- 在 GSM8K 上拟合 IRT → 用估计的 θ 预测 ARC/HellaSwag/MMLU 表现
47	- 5,000+ 模型同时出现在多个 benchmark，可直接做 cross-benchmark equating
48	- **零 GPU 需求，立即可行**
49	
50	**方案 B: GSM1K 聚合验证（可做，精度有限）**
51	- 从论文/leaderboard 提取 ~30-50 个模型的 GSM1K 准确率
52	- 匹配这些模型在 METABENCH GSM8K 中的 IRT θ 估计值
53	- 检验 θ 是否预测 GSM1K 准确率（不受 contamination 影响的泛化能力）
54	- **零 GPU，但 N 较小（~30-50）**
55	
56	**方案 C: 自行评测 GSM1K（最彻底，需 GPU）**
57	- 下载 GSM1K 题目，用 lm-evaluation-harness 对 METABENCH 中的开源模型跑评测
58	- 构建 GSM1K response matrix，与 GSM8K IRT 参数做 equating
59	- **需要大量 GPU（数百个模型 × 1,205 题），估计 ~100-500 GPU 小时**
60	- 不推荐作为第一步
61	
62	### Blocking Requirements
63	
64	无 blocking requirements。方案 A 可立即启动。
65	
66	### Contamination Detection 附加价值
67	
68	METABENCH 数据天然支持 contamination 分析：
69	- GSM1K 论文已标记 Phi/Mistral 等家族有 contamination evidence
70	- IRT 可以从 response pattern 内部检测异常（item-person fit），不需要外部 GSM1K 数据
71	- 两种方法可交叉验证：IRT misfit 指标 vs GSM8K-GSM1K accuracy gap
72	
73	## 建议行动
74	
75	1. **立即开始**：用 METABENCH GSM8K 数据拟合 2PL IRT 模型
76	2. **同步进行**：对 ARC/HellaSwag/TruthfulQA/Winogrande 也拟合 IRT，做 cross-benchmark equating
77	3. **后续验证**：提取 GSM1K 论文数据做方案 B 验证
78	4. **可选扩展**：如果需要更强的 GSM1K 证据，再考虑方案 C
79	
```

lm-evaluation-harness 对 METABENCH 中的开源模型跑评测\n- 构建 GSM1K response matrix，与 GSM8K IRT 参数做 equating\n- **需要大量 GPU（数百个模型 × 1,205 题），估计 ~100-500 GPU 小时**\n- 不推荐作为第一步\n\n### Blocking Requirements\n\n无 ARC/HellaSwag/TruthfulQA/Winogrande 也拟合 IRT，做 cross-benchmark equating\n3. **后续验证**：提取 GSM1K 论文数据做方案 B 验证\n4. **可选扩展**：如果需要更强的 GSM1K 证据，再考虑方案 C\n",

_source line 1025 · 2026-05-21T00:15:08.036Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a data availability investigation for plan_004 (cross-benchmark validation). Key findings:

1. **METABENCH has GSM8K data**: 1,319 items × 6,702 models — immediately usable
2. **GSM1K limitations**: Only aggregated accuracy for ~30-50 models, no per-item response matrix
3. **Three plans proposed**: A (internal cross-validation), B (GSM1K aggregated), C (run GSM1K ourselves)

My assessment:

**方案 A (Internal cross-benchmark validation)** is the most practical and immediately actionable. 

But I'm realizing there's a disconnect between what I proposed and what I actually recommended. My core question is whether DIF analysis on GSM8K can detect contamination that shows up as a gap between GSM8K and GSM1K performance. Plan A tests whether DIF patterns generalize across different benchmarks, which is valuable but doesn't directly address contamination detection. Plan B would let me match the 30-50 models we have in both datasets and compare their IRT parameters. by model to identify contaminated ones. The core challenge is that DIF analysis works on groups, not individual models, and since GSM8K has been public since 2021, essentially all models could be contaminated to some degree — similar to what happened with MMLU. So I need to think about whether we can actually define meaningful temporal cohorts where one group is clearly more contaminated than another, or if we should focus on the GSM1K gap as the primary signal for identifying which models show signs of contamination.

Looking at the METABENCH data, we have 6,702 models with GSM8K scores — actually more than the MMLU dataset — so a temporal split between 2023 and 2024 models could work. The real advantage here is that GSM1K gives us an independent measure of contamination through the accuracy gap, so if DIF flags the same items where that gap is largest, that would be genuine validation. The limitation is having only 30-50 models with GSM1K scores, which constrains statistical power for this comparison.

I should prioritize running temporal DIF on GSM8K first to see if the 31-38% pattern holds more broadly, then use GSM1K aggregate validation as a secondary check even with the smaller sample size.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is a very important result. Let me analyze the GSM8K DIF findings:

**GSM8K Temporal DIF Results:**
- Standard matching (purified GSM8K total): 18.9% C → lower than MMLU's 31.3%
- External matching (4 other benchmarks): 47.4% C → much HIGHER
- Sanity check: 0.3% → method works correctly
- Standard vs External correlation: ρ=0.926 → consistent pattern

This is interesting and somewhat surprising. Let me think about what this means:

1. **Standard matching 18.9% < MMLU 31.3%**: GSM8K has less temporal DIF than MMLU with standard matching. This could be because:
   - Math ability might improve more uniformly across items rather than showing differential patterns
   - GSM8K's smaller item pool (1,319 vs 12,508) gives less statistical power to detect DIF
   - The items themselves may be more homogeneous in their difficulty and discriminative properties

2. **External matching 47.4% >> Standard matching 18.9%**: This massive 28.5 percentage point gap is striking. The key insight is that using the purified GSM8K total score for stratification partially absorbs the contamination effect—since contaminated items inflate both the matching variable and the outcome, this masks the true DIF. External matching sidesteps this by stratifying on completely independent benchmark scores, revealing the actual temporal DIF that was being underestimated.

But wait, this contradicts what we found in plan_011. There, standard MH actually had a lower baseline FPR than external matching, and when we injected contamination, the matching variable's FPR inflated dramatically. So contamination should amplify DIF detection, not suppress it. The worker's explanation seems backwards relative to that experiment. I need to reconsider what's actually happening here—there's something about how contamination affects the matching variable differently depending on whether we're measuring DIF on the contaminated items themselves versus the broader population.

Actually, I think I see the distinction now. In plan_011, we were measuring DIF on specific injected items while the matching variable got contaminated by those same items. But in the GSM8K case, if contamination is spread uniformly across all items, then standard matching absorbs that uniform shift in the matching variable, which would indeed make individual item DIF appear smaller. With external matching using independent benchmarks, that uniform contamination signal isn't absorbed, so genuine item-level differences show up more clearly. Though there's still the possibility that external matching is just a worse ability control overall, which could inflate false positives rather than reveal true DIF.

The real question is whether that 47.4% contamination rate with external matching reflects actual item-level contamination or whether it's residual ability differences between the 2023 and 2024 cohorts that the external benchmarks don't fully capture. I saw something similar in plan_016 where cross-subject matching had higher false positive rates than within-subject matching because total scores don't perfectly capture ability within a specific domain. The same mechanism could be at play here.

Looking at the DIF direction, there's a 2:1 ratio favoring 2024 models over 2023, which aligns with what I'd expect if 2024 models are genuinely stronger.

The temporal DIF pattern on GSM8K (18.9% baseline, higher with external matching) suggests this isn't just an MMLU artifact—temporal DIF appears across benchmarks. But I need to check a few things: how exactly are they constructing the external matching variable across those four other benchmarks, how do they handle models missing scores on some benchmarks, and whether the different cohort sizes (1017 vs 804 for GSM8K versus 1080 vs 807 for MMLU) affect the conclusions. Most importantly, I need to determine if the external matching approach is actually introducing ability confounding rather than reducing it.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Reviewer for the `irt_equated_bench_regen` project. My role is to independently verify experimental conclusions, review research design, and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I operate read-only, cannot execute experiments, and must respond in Chinese. This session continued from a compacted conversation covering plans 001-014 and the project's pivot from "DIF as contamination detector" to "psychometric characterization of LLM benchmark item instability." In this session, I reviewed: plan_013 (Q3 diagnostics), plan_016 (within-subject semi-synthetic + revision), plan_017 (downstream impact), plan_004 step 1 (GSM8K cross-benchmark DIF), multiple paper figures, experiment summary, literature survey, artifacts QC, and two Director-requested strategic evaluations (Claim strength / ICLR viability).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MH-DIF (Mantel-Haenszel Differential Item Functioning)**: Core detection method comparing item performance across temporal model cohorts (2023 vs 2024), stratified by ability
   - **ETS C Classification**: Effect-size threshold |Δ_MH|≥1.5 + significance — primary detection criterion (Decision D001)
   - **Within-subject vs Cross-subject matching**: Within uses same-subject purified total score; cross uses other subjects' total score. Cross-subject ΔFPR=0.000 (matching variable not contaminated by injection)
   - **Matching variable contamination**: When contaminated items are in the matching variable, FPR inflates. plan_011 showed 0.298→0.616 for standard MH; plan_016 confirmed +0.067 to +0.281 at subject level
   - **Semi-synthetic validation (ΔTPR)**: Inject known differential contamination into real response matrix, measure TPR increase. ΔTPR controls for baseline FPR
   - **Global vs differential contamination**: Li et al. web-overlap = global (all models share), DIF detects differential (group-specific). Enrichment≈1.0 explained by this distinction
   - **Detection trilemma**: IRT fit ↔ power ↔ confound control — empirical tradeoff from three failed structural improvements
   - **Yen's Q3 statistic**: Residual correlations between item pairs after θ-conditioning. |Q3|>0.20 = significant local dependence
   - **METABENCH**: 5,227 models × 12,508 MMLU items (Kipnis et al., ICLR 2025); also contains GSM8K 6,702 models × 1,319 items
   - **External matching for GSM8K**: Uses average proportion correct across ARC, HellaSwag, TruthfulQA, Winogrande as matching variable — completely independent of GSM8K items
   - **Downstream impact (plan_017)**: DIF item removal barely changes rankings (ρ≈0.997) but causes MORE disruption than random removal (z=-8 to -49σ) — DIF items are structured information carriers, not noise
   - **Negative-framing-review**: Four-dimensional review skill (rationalization / evidence / narrative / honesty) for evaluating story pivot proposals

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`artifacts/plan_013/plan_013_report.md`**
     - Q3 local independence diagnostic results
     - Within-subject mean Q3=0.0086, cross-subject=0.0031, 11.3% vs 2.2% >0.20 threshold
     - Mann-Whitney p=2.29e-9, moral_scenarios worst (34.2% >0.20)
     - Conclusion: mild LD, not primary driver of 31% FPR

I had identified several issues to flag in my review:

   1. The standard→external gap (18.9%→47.4%) is much larger than expected. The worker's explanation that "purified total score absorbs contamination" contradicts plan_011's finding where standard MH FPR INFLATED (not deflated) under contamination. The alternative explanation — external benchmarks don't perfectly capture GSM8K ability, creating residual confounding — mirrors what we saw in plan_016 where cross-subject baseline FPR was higher than within-subject for some subjects.

   2. Standard matching 18.9% < MMLU's 31.3% — GSM8K has less temporal DIF, possibly because math ability improves more uniformly across items.

   3. The Δ_MH distribution is very different: standard mean=0.228, external mean=1.467. The external matching dramatically amplifies effect sizes, which could be genuine signal or ability confounding.

   4. Sanity check 0.3% confirms method validity.

   5. DIF direction 2:1 favoring 2024 is consistent with capability improvement pattern seen in MMLU.

{
  "parentUuid": "cf71bb28-eb49-4d00-ad2f-9a688fb63db7",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/artifacts/plan_004_data_availability.md",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520_2/projects/irt_equated_bench_regen/artifacts/plan_004_data_availability.md",
        "content": "# plan_004 Data Availability Report\n\nDate: 2026-05-21\n\n## METABENCH Coverage\n\nMETABENCH (Kipnis et al., ICLR 2025) 包含 Open LLM Leaderboard 的全部 6 个 benchmarks，数据格式为 per-item binary responses (source, item, correct)。\n\n| Benchmark   | Items   | Models | Total Rows  |\n|-------------|---------|--------|-------------|\n| GSM8K       | 1,319   | 6,702  | 8,839,939   |\n| ARC         | 1,172   | 5,227  | 6,126,045   |\n| HellaSwag   | 10,042  | 5,227  | 52,489,535  |\n| TruthfulQA  | 817     | 5,228  | 4,271,277   |\n| Winogrande  | 1,267   | 6,558  | 8,308,987   |\n| MMLU (57 subjects) | ~14,000+ | ~5,000+ | 74,012,560 |\n\n- **GSM8K present: YES**\n- GSM8K items: 1,319 (full test set)\n- GSM8K models: 6,702 (覆盖面极广，包含 Yi, Phi, Mistral, Llama, Qwen 等主流家族)\n- 数据位置: `artifacts/metabench_data/benchmark-data/gsm8k.csv`\n- Prompts: `artifacts/metabench_data/benchmark-data/gsm8k_prompts.csv`\n\n## GSM1K Availability\n\n| 数据类型 | 可用性 | 来源 |\n|---------|--------|------|\n| 题目文本 (1,205 problems) | ✅ 公开 | [HuggingFace ScaleAI/gsm1k](https://huggingface.co/datasets/ScaleAI/gsm1k) |\n| Per-model accuracy (~30-50 models) | ✅ 公开 | [论文 arXiv:2405.00332](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00332) + [Scale Labs Leaderboard](https://labs.scale.com/leaderboard/math) |\n| Per-item binary responses (thousands of models) | ❌ 不公开 | Scale AI 内部评测，未公开 response matrix |\n\n关键限制：GSM1K 没有像 METABENCH 那样的大规模 per-item response matrix。论文只公布了约 30-50 个模型的聚合准确率。\n\n## Feasibility Assessment\n\n### Can proceed immediately: YES\n\nMETABENCH 的 GSM8K 数据完全满足 IRT 分析需求：\n- 1,319 items × 6,702 models 的完整 binary response matrix\n- 同时拥有 5 个其他 benchmark 的同等数据\n\n### Cross-Benchmark Validation 方案\n\n**方案 A: 内部交叉验证（推荐，无需额外数据）**\n- 用 METABENCH 内部的 6 个 benchmark 做交叉验证\n- 在 GSM8K 上拟合 IRT → 用估计的 θ 预测 ARC/HellaSwag/MMLU 表现\n- 5,000+ 模型同时出现在多个 benchmark，可直接做 cross-benchmark equating\n- **零 GPU 需求，立即可行**\n\n**方案 B: GSM1K 聚合验证（可做，精度有限）**\n- 从论文/leaderboard 提取 ~30-50 个模型的 GSM1K 准确率\n- 匹配这些模型在 METABENCH GSM8K 中的 IRT θ 估计值\n- 检验 θ 是否预测 GSM1K 准确率（不受 contamination 影响的泛化能力）\n- **零 GPU，但 N 较小（~30-50）**\n\n**方案 C: 自行评测 GSM1K（最彻底，需 GPU）**\n- 下载 GSM1K 题目，用 lm-evaluation-harness 对 METABENCH 中的开源模型跑评测\n- 构建 GSM1K response matrix，与 GSM8K IRT 参数做 equating\n- **需要大量 GPU（数百个模型 × 1,205 题），估计 ~100-500 GPU 小时**\n- 不推荐作为第一步\n\n### Blocking Requirements\n\n无 blocking requirements。方案 A 可立即启动。\n\n### Contamination Detection 附加价值\n\nMETABENCH 数据天然支持 contamination 分析：\n- GSM1K 论文已标记 Phi/Mistral 等家族有 contamination evidence\n- IRT 可以从 response pattern 内部检测异常（item-person fit），不需要外部 GSM1K 数据\n- 两种方法可交叉验证：IRT misfit 指标 vs GSM8K-GSM1K accuracy gap\n\n## 建议行动\n\n1. **立即开始**：用 METABENCH GSM8K 数据拟合 2PL IRT 模型\n2. **同步进行**：对 ARC/HellaSwag/TruthfulQA/Winogrande 也拟合 IRT，做 cross-benchmark equating\n3. **后续验证**：提取 GSM1K 论文数据做方案 B 验证\n4. **可选扩展**：如果需要更强的 GSM1K 证据，再考虑方案 C\n",
        "numLines": 79,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 79
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/plan_004_data_availability.md"
  },

I need to post my review for plan_004 step 1 GSM8K temporal DIF results. I had already read the report and identified key issues. Let me finalize my analysis and post it.

Key points from my analysis:

1. GSM8K shows temporal DIF (18.9% C standard, 47.4% C external), confirming cross-benchmark generalizability
2. The standard→external gap (18.9%→47.4%) needs careful interpretation
3. The worker's explanation that "purified total score absorbs contamination" is questionable — in plan_011, standard MH FPR INFLATED under contamination, not deflated

5. The 2:1 DIF direction favoring 2024 aligns with what I saw in the MMLU pattern

Now I should check the registry to confirm the experiment status and specifications before posting my review.
